The Soma mining disaster has also led to public opinion on mining activities and business health and safety in Turkey. The fact that workers' work safety and labor health in the mines have been neglected and run on low wages and long hours has become more than a sectoral issue. Such practices as privatisation, subcontractorization, redevance, and unclehood have developed a social awareness that adequate work safety measures are not taken and that the failure of control in particular is causing business accidents to increase. This painting, which placed Turkey in the third place in Europe, third place in the world, and again ranked first in mortal mining geese in the world, has become crucial in business accidents. The May 13 Soma disaster, which has raised a profound public impression of Turkey and the world, requires serious questioning of mining activities in our country. In this direction, on 21/5/2014, the Commission for Assembly Research was established in Soma County for the purposes of investigating both the May 13th mining disaster and other mining accidents and the measures needed to be taken for occupational health and occupational safety. The Commission's report, which began its work on 4/6/2014, was distributed on 30/1/2015 with the number of 680 rows. HDP Group Vice President HDP Group Vice President Deep state debates in Turkey have been a topic of debate for decades. It is also known that the deep state operates in the area in which Kurds live intensively, especially during the conflict that has been developed due to the unresolved problem of the Kurdish problem, signing inhumane practices in their activities. Deep-state debates that have been rekindled recently are wanted to be seen as a past reckoning. The Kurds, on the other hand, are facing deep states and extensions as they were yesterday, suffering all kinds of pain. The best way to expose the deep state in its practice is to change the official ideology that creates deep-state structures, change the accountability, a process of confrontation. The deep state formation of the 1990s was considered the cause of death of the Kurdish identity, which the practice imposed on the people of the region, by rough lines, by mass graves, unknowns, displaced and ignored by the official ideology. Although it has been claimed to be facing the deep state in terms of Turkey's democracy, it is not even admissible for Kurds. The point is that deep government practices that existed during that period are now being implemented in the region. The mass graves that exist in the region have not been uncovered and no serious steps have been taken. The unsub used to be carried out by the anti-corruption organization of government officials, while today, people are being shot in the street and there is no serious legal action against public officials who kill. As a result of the transfer of a political issue to the safety concept, the district and its centers are rapidly ejaculated. After all, nothing has changed for Kurds in the state-deep state dilemma since 1924. Between 1924 and 1938, the reform operations led to village evacuations in the 1990s, while as of 2015, the paramilitarian esedullah teams were launched under the name of the clean-up operations. Above all, and perhaps the unchanging of the monotheistic/reciprocal understanding of official ideology, which is the source of such practices, is also the basis for the political legitimacy of policies implemented by the deep government today. Every separatist language used by politicians from the ruling party moves deep structures, and the wave of violence against Kurds is growing. It is a recent popular opinion in Kurdish public that the deep state continues to impose inhuman practices on Kurds without need of depth. Turkey to the chairman of the Grand National Assembly In order to ensure that many public institutions and institutions work in our country, subcontractors, 4/C personnel, temporary workers, temporary staff and contracted personnel will be put on staff, provide necessary rights and permanent solutions to the issues of our Constitution 98th, Turkish Grand National Assembly, and 105th Articles, we respectfully submit to the opening of Parliament research. 29/12/2015 [[İstanbul]] [[İzmir]] 3) Ahmet Kenan Tanrıkulu [[İzmir]] 4) Edip Semih Yalçın [[İstanbul]] [[Scholarship]] [[İstanbul]] [[Gaziantep]] [[Scholarship]] [[Adana]] [[Afyonkarahisar]] [[Aydın]] [[İstanbul]] [[Kayseri]] [[Mersin]] [[Osmaniye]] [[Ankara]] 17) Ahmet Selim Yurdakul [[Antalya]] [[Sakarya]] [[Ankara]] [[Ankara]] [[Adana]] 22) Ekmeleddin Mehmet İhsanoğlu [[İstanbul]] In our country, many public institutions and institutions employ subcontractors, 4/CL personnel, temporary workers, temporary staff and contract staff. According to data from the State Chief of Staff November 2015, there are 218,529 temporary employees, temporary staff and 585,788 subcontractors, according to the Department of Labor and Social Security data. In our country, a significant portion of public service is made available to subcontractors. The subcontractorship system, which was legalized in the early days of AKP governments in 2003 with the concept of "service service," operates today against the Turkish economy and the Turkish social security system. Business security and social security rights for employees are also severely undermined, as the quality of public services, which has been transferred to the private sector through the subcontractory system. The subcontractor system, which began with the AKP's rule, has become widely perceived as "steal exploitation." This system is viewed as a means of supplying cheap labor and subcontractors to subcontractors and creates a sense that it is being removed from the principle of rationality in public administration. Currently in public, 585,788 subcontractors -- sub-employees -- problems are increasing every day for sub-contractors. We can list some of these as follows: the failure of workers to receive full and regular wages; the fact that public authorities do not have the obligation to check whether the sub-employee pays the employee's salary, except for certain jobs given to the sub-employee; the fact that the sub-employee is unable to obtain annual allowance due to the change of employer; the lower employers do not have an annual allowance; the underprivilegation of job health and safety measures is expected from the employer; the fact that they expect to receive job health and safety measures from the employer, and that they do not need to care for the work and training; the fact that workers who work for sub-employment and that they have no legal access to their own employersses; and that they can't get out of work structure; and that they can't get out of employment through the years; they can't get out of employment agreement; they can't get out of employment, and they can't get out of employment, even if they can't get out of any kind of employment agreement; they can't get out of any kind; they can't get out of employment, even a loans, even if they can't get out of the year; they can't get out of employment, and severance; they can't get out of employment agreements, and severance by a job, even a contract, and severance. These problems are also mentioned in the report by the relevant Ministry on "sub-employment, temporary employment and remote work." In addition, employees in Article 4 (c) of Law No. 657 receive a much lower salary than those in contract or on staff who do the same work. The staff in this area are not granted financial and social benefits, such as family, child welfare, overwork, production incentives, additional payment. This is clearly against the principle of "equal pay for equal work" which is adopted as an internal rule of law. Along with these, the temporary workers, which we refer to as temporary workers, are employed for five months and twenty-nine days a year at the most, leaving on a hundred and seventy-nine days so that they do not receive a staff. In our country, where salary workers work 12 months a year have difficulty living, it is not possible for workers who work five months and twenty-nine days at the most in temporary status. Temporary workers who work for the future are concerned about the provision and future of their families. Seasonal workers, insured for five months and twenty-nine days a year, are having major problems in terms of social security. "The government takes the necessary measures to raise the life level of employees, to protect employees and unemployed people, to support work, to create an economic environment that is suitable to prevent unemployment, and to ensure the peace of work." It's shaped like this. In view of all these issues, it is clear that despite constitutional principles and international agreements, there is no healthy and consistent personnel policy in our country. In this context, a parliamentary investigation is required to ensure that subcontractors, staffers, temporary workers, temporary staff, and contract personnel will be admitted to the staff and the rights required, and the responsibility of the social state will be met. It has been presented to your knowledge. The motions will take their place on the agenda, and talks on whether or not to open general discussion and parliamentary research will be held at the appropriate time. Mr. President, I'd like to read the 1st and 2nd Articles of the Venice Commission Report in the conclusion section. Article 1 states that the basic principles for judicial independence must be held in the constitution or in equivalent documents. Among these are the principles that the judiciary is independent of other state forces, that judges are only subject to law, that judges are separated only because of their different duties, and that, of course, judges cannot be replaced. Now, I'd like to ask: Is there anything incompatible with the principles adopted by the Venice Commission in 2011 and 2014? Of course not. The 2nd Article of the Commission Report is as follows: All decisions regarding the occupations and careers of judges must be taken within the framework of the law, by looking after the truth and based on objective standards.
